# Legacy inventory: every system of the legacy build

**Purpose.** R7 of the v2 phase 0 brief (`docs/V2_PHASE0_BRIEF.md` §4): "Every system of the current game: what it
does, where it lives, the evidence and numbers we have, and a pre-marked recommendation (port to TypeScript /
rewrite in Rust / redesign / drop) with a reason." The other research topics (R1–R6) read it. Its tuning numbers are
in `docs/LEGACY_TUNING.md` and `docs/legacy-tuning.json`.

**Marks:** each one is Claude's recommendation with a reason; Mustafa confirms or changes it in the last column.
- **rewrite in Rust**: a mechanic the authoritative v2 simulation decides and LOOP_V2 keeps (movement, collision,
  shots, line of sight, damage, team filtering, bot navigation and behaviour, vehicle physics). A rule LOOP_V2 marks
  [OPEN] says "rule pending LOOP_V2 [OPEN]".
- **port to TypeScript**: client code worth keeping in shape (input, camera, procedural builders, cosmetic animation,
  synthesized audio, HUD and menu flows), re-expressed for the new client.
- **redesign**: something v2 already plans to change. Where LOOP_V2 replaces a legacy rule, the row cites its section
  (eras and income, silos and tech boxes, win conditions, spawning and buying, progression); the map editor follows
  EDITOR_V2; the look pipeline is "redesign (look brief pending)" and nothing more.
- **drop**: dead code and legacy-only workarounds (classic-script globals, the symbol-conservation checks, r128
  limits).
- **Look brief pending**: every row whose output a player sees carries it, whatever its mark. For those rows the
  mark is about the code technique only, never the appearance: the v2 look is not decided (Mustafa and the Advisor
  write the look brief).

**Tally of recommended marks (247 rows):** rewrite in Rust 45 · port to TypeScript 74 · redesign 96 · drop 32. Look brief pending on 118 rows. Per area: the table under "Tally by area" at the end.

**Date:** 2026-10-27 (read from 2026-10-24). **Code:** main at f2ccc13. The engine files (`shamble-core.js`, `shamble-game.js`,
`shamble-editor.js`), both pages and `tools/` are byte-identical to 48ffb51, where the reading started, so their line
numbers hold on both. Kit rows (KT, and the TL rows on `.claude/` files) describe the kit at 48ffb51, as plan condition
8 asks: kit-v3-0 (PR #26) rewrote those files since, so their line numbers are 48ffb51's (`git show 48ffb51:<file>`) and
each row that kit-v3-0 changed says so. In the Where column, `core`, `game` and `editor` mean the three engine files.
Doc citations (STATUS, ROADMAP, the reports and the other docs) are to main at f2ccc13 too. PR #25 (merged on
2026-10-27 as 4c24d07) has since changed STATUS.md and ROADMAP.md: STATUS lines from 37 on are 0 to 2 higher today,
ROADMAP lines from 49 on 1 to 3 higher; `git show f2ccc13:docs/STATUS.md` shows the text as cited. Rows whose fact
that PR changed (main's branch protection) say so.

**How each state was checked:**
- **[C]** (141 rows): code read at main; every Where re-opened by a second reader.
- **[M]** (106 rows): the row also cites a measurement recorded in the repository and names it: a report under
  `docs/reports/`, the STATUS health table, `docs/AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md`, the `docs/ANIMATION_PLAN.md` §6 metrics, or a
  harness in `tools/`. The measurements were not re-run, except where a row says so; the final review re-read each at its source.
- No row rests on a doc claim alone ([D]): every row was read in the code.

**How this file was made.** Six passes, each by Opus agents at effort high (xhigh for the engine and netcode reviews), with notes on checkpoints:
1. Read: one reader per area (15) wrote 240 rows, each citing file:line.
2. Verify: one skeptic per area re-opened every Where and every number and tried to refute it: 97
   confirmed, 143 corrected, 0 refuted. They also listed 68 systems no row covered.
3. Coverage: a critic parsed the three engine files (acorn) and listed every top-level declaration no row names:
   core 138/138, game 584/714, editor 204/353 named before the edit pass.
4. Edit and re-verify: one editor per area applied every correction, added or widened rows for the missing systems
   and the critic's suggestions (9 rows added, 45 widened, 73 suggestions not taken,
   each with a reason), and a second skeptic checked every added or widened row (13 confirmed,
   41 corrected, 0 refuted).
5. Apply and check: one applier per area turned the 41 corrections into exact edits, a script applied them,
   and one checker per area compared every change with its correction and the code (6 more fixes, applied).
   One session edit: KT14 records the night-shift routine set up on 2026-10-25.
6. Final review: five verifiers re-read every [M] row's measurement at its source and 28 sampled code-read rows;
   the engine-architect, netcode-architect, graphics-engineer and game-designer reviewed the marks and notes of their areas;
   a coverage closer placed the declarations no row covered. 115 findings: 110 fixed (3 only in part, and
   1 rejected, because kit line numbers stay at 48ffb51, see above), 4 handled by the session (the
   coverage count, the [M] definition, two duplicate rows merged); one checker per area then checked every fix and
   rejection (13 more fixes). The rounds stopped there, at the Advisor's word (2026-10-27).
   Coverage after these passes (top-level declarations named in some row's Where, mechanical match): core 138/138, game 625/714, editor 293/353; every other one sits inside a line range a row cites (see "Not covered and not verified").

The notes of every pass are in the history of the R7 branch (`.checkpoints/r7-legacy-inventory/`, removed in
the last commit before review, as `.claude/CHECKPOINTS.md` §9 requires).

## Contents

- **Core (`shamble-core.js`):** CD. Core data, tuning and persistence (21) · CL. Core look pipeline (14) · CC. Core characters, factions and guns (11) · CM. Core maps and building kit (13) · CS. Core headless SIM math (11)
- **Game (`shamble-game.js`):** GW. Game world, map building and movement physics (21) · GA. Game animation (ANIM v2) (16) · GC. Game combat, teams and vehicles (19) · GN. Game bots and navigation (14) · GE. Game economy, objectives and progression (17) · GP. Game presentation, input and dispatch (16)
- **Editor, pages, tools and kit:** ED. Editor (`shamble-editor.js`) (16) · PG. Pages (`index.html`, `editor.html`) (20) · TL. Tools (the verification toolkit) (17) · KT. Kit (process) (21)
- **Tally by area**
- **Not covered and not verified**

| CL14 | userData tag contract between builders and the look pipeline | The silent contract that decides how every mesh is styled and merged. Builders set mesh userData tags: matKind picks the cel ramp, outlineW the ink weight, part makes it a character part at 0.8x ink (CHAR_OUTLINE), noOutline skips the hull. Two tags live on the MATERIAL's userData instead: nature (with baseCol) marks environment materials that get the nature brightness gain, which toonify copies onto the new toon material and the static merge folds into its material key. Static-piece placement sets noMerge on a collider body to keep it out of the static merge. The pipeline sets its own: isOutline on every hull, _toon once a mesh is converted. A missing or misspelt tag fails silently: the mesh just gets the default ramp, the world ink weight, a hull it should not have, or the wrong brightness. | Set on meshes by builders: matKind shamble-core.js:687 (buildEntity, from the recipe's mat role) and :754 (_gunParts); outlineW :686, :716 (makeAMKRifle), :755; part :686, :716, :753, shamble-game.js:1200 ('jug'); noOutline :276 (applyVillain visor), :689 (copied from a recipe part's noOutline, e.g. :482), :716, :755, shamble-game.js:3237 (FXPool 'casing'); noMerge shamble-game.js:577, :583, :598. Set on materials: nature :230 (sea MeshBasicMaterial, which also sets its own baseCol), :1414 (hedge leaf), :1417 (bank), shamble-game.js:3070 (tree leaf and bark); toonify at :284 copies nature onto the toon material and gives it a fresh baseCol (its colour before the nature gain). Set by the pipeline: isOutline :395 (addHull); _toon :384 (merged mesh), :415 (stylizeOne). Read: matKind :404, :415; outlineW and noOutline :405, :416; part :392 (CHAR_OUTLINE, :242 = 0.8); nature and baseCol :221 (applyEnv re-tint), :284 (toonify), :357-359 (_matSig merge key); noMerge :364; _toon :413; isOutline :271 (applyVillain), :364, :366, :386, :401, :413, :646 (applyFaction), shamble-game.js:245, :2573, :2575, shamble-editor.js:814, :877, :882. | Code read (grep of each tag across the three engine files at 48ffb51). No measurement recorded. Related bugs come from the stylize pass, not the tags themselves (CL8): PR #8, editor Ctrl+Z / Reset / Load left rebuilt pieces on plain materials with no ink (docs/reports/2026-10-14-editor-undo-ground.md:4, :13-14); in three-teams 3a a reviewer's fix as first written would have left the 7 bots parked at boot unstyled. Review caught it, and it was fixed in 5695e56 before it shipped (docs/reports/2026-10-16-three-teams-core-steps-1-6.md:88-91). | [C] | Learn: per-mesh intent (material kind, ink weight, skip outline, environment-only brightening, keep out of merge) is real data the look needs. Avoid: carrying it as untyped, optional userData strings that a later sweep reads, with no check that a builder set them; a typo or missing tag shows only as a wrong look. In v2, if the look brief keeps any of these, they belong as typed fields on the builder's output (or the material key), set where the mesh is built. Ties CL4 (toonify reads matKind and nature), CL6 (addHull reads part), CL8 (stylize reads noOutline and outlineW, sets _toon) and CL9 (merge reads isOutline and noMerge). | **redesign** — Look pipeline (styling inputs): redesign (look brief pending), with no further recommendation. *Look brief pending.* |

Baseline (headless eval with tools/preview.js on the booted game, counting each soldier's group): a styled bot is 122-140 meshes, that is 66-77 primitive parts plus 56-64 ink hulls (the player 154); two of a bot's parts are the unlit health-bar planes (MeshBasicMaterial, game:797-798), and after stylizeScene every other part has its own toon material (distinct materials = parts; core:404). The legacy code has no SkinnedMesh, InstancedMesh, BatchedMesh or LOD (grep over the three engine files: 0 hits). The parts animate independently, so the static merge (mergeStaticGroup, core:360-388, which bakes each part's transform into one geometry) cannot apply. Merging per material needs rigid skinning (one bone per part) or BatchedMesh with a transform per part; a spike measures draw calls and GPU time for each option at 48 soldiers on real hardware. The shield accent is a hard-coded teal 0x38e1b0, not a faction colour. Each prop makes its own materials. In LOOP_V2 §4 and §5 the Riot Shield and Banzai blade are class abilities unlocked at Tech 1. Whether the shield blocks shots is a simulation rule; these props only show it. The block flash is broken (confirmed by headless eval, no render). userData.boss (core:1471) holds the pre-toon MeshStandardMaterial. stylizeScene (core:404) and stylizeOne (core:415) replace every mesh material with a new toonify copy (emissive cloned, core:281), so game:2746 sets emissiveIntensity on a material no mesh renders while NPR is on (core:239 NPR=true). No WEAPONS entry has id 'sword' either, so the sword shows only for the katana. GA11 (area GA) owns the holds that use these props. Keep it cosmetic: no hit decision comes from these meshes (all noRay). Merged from CM14 on 2026-10-27, which listed the same builders from the CM line range. | **port to TypeScript** — Client-side procedural props for kept abilities (shield, blade). Drop the unused axe. *Look brief pending.* |

A NaN ray counts as blocked only when maxT is NaN as well (as in hasLOS with a NaN end point, and only if at least one live static exists); with a finite maxT, an all-NaN direction misses everything (a ray with one or two NaN components can still hit through the slabs that are left). | shamble-core.js:1529-1543 rayStatics(S,ox,oy,oz,dx,dy,dz,maxT,anyHit,out); comment at :1526-1528. | CS7 | Hitscan shot resolution (resolveShot) | A pure query that never changes the world. It casts a shot ray against every live soldier (head, torso and legs), skipping the shooter and, unless friendly fire is on, the shooter's team. Then it tests the static colliders and a flat ground plane, and returns the nearest hit: point, normal, kind, id and body part. The result object is reused on every call. The result record is the contract between the SIM and the game: t, hit point (px, py, pz), unit normal (nx, ny, nz), kind, id, part and mult. kind is one of none, enemy (any soldier, teammates included), building, harvester or block (the ground plane and every other static: crates, walls, platforms, ramps, houses, cover, trees and props). id is the SOLDIERS slot for enemy, the buildings[] index for building, the harvesters[] index for harvester, and -1 for block and none. The game maps a hit back by kind and id (SOLDIERS[r.id], buildings[r.id], harvesters[r.id]). Team is checked again at the call site for buildings and harvesters, and for soldiers inside applyDamage (game:1512). Bolts stick to whatever they hit with no second team check. | shamble-core.js:1493-1510 resolveShot(world,shooterId,ox,oy,oz,dx,dy,dz,range,shooterTeam) (comment at :1491-1492; world._res and world._nrm are pooled; ground plane at world.groundY only when dy<0). AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md:25: resolveShot was 13 % of the original ~8 MB/s GC churn. AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md:111: about 1.5 KB per call even when every ray misses (before B7). AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md:25 (P1): Math.hypot was 12.5 % of the original GC churn (the code comment at core:741 calls it 'the #1 source', but the audit puts updateOcclusion and updateSoldierAnim higher at 17 %). | [M] | In Rust use the vector type's length (no allocation question). | CS11 | Headless SIM fence (the lift-out boundary) | A comment fence marks the bottom of core as a pure maths block with no THREE or DOM use, kept contiguous so it can be lifted out whole for a later headless simulation (Phase 6). The file header and MODULE_MAP point to it. The block is in fact free of THREE and DOM, but it is not self-contained: BODY reads CHAR_DEFAULT from the character recipe higher up in core, resolveShot and hasLOS call hyp3 from outside the fence, hasLOS reads the global SIM instead of taking the world as an argument, and the VIEW-to-SIM bridge comment and the SIM global sit inside the fenced range. The two lists of what the fence holds disagree: the core header omits makeInputCommand, rayStatic and rayStatics; MODULE_MAP omits BTN and makeInputCommand. | shamble-core.js:1474-1478 fence header ('HEADLESS SIM MATH (pure: zero THREE/DOM)', Phase-6 lift-out target); :1512-1515 VIEW <-> SIM adapter comment and :1516 SIM inside the fenced range; file header pointer core:18-20 (list of fenced symbols) and core:25-26 (not headless-runnable today); docs/MODULE_MAP.md:120-124. Dependencies that cross the fence: core:1482 BODY={...CHAR_DEFAULT.hit} (CHAR_DEFAULT at core:421), hyp3 core:744 used at core:1508 and :1544, hasLOS core:1544 reads SIM.statics. | none recorded (no report measures or tests the fence). Code read: a grep of core:1474-1544 for THREE, document, window, Math.random, performance and Date finds only the two comment lines that name THREE, so the block is pure of THREE and DOM. MODULE_MAP.md:122-124 states core is not headless-runnable today and that the lift-out is future work. | [C] | Keep the idea: the simulation lives behind a hard boundary that has no rendering or browser dependency, so the same code runs in the browser and on the server. In v2 that boundary is the Rust simulation crate compiled to Wasm and native (V2_PHASE0_BRIEF.md:7 and :121, R1; repository layout as a Cargo workspace, :125, R5), enforced by the compiler instead of a comment. Learn from the leaks here: shape data (BODY) came from the visual recipe, helpers came from outside the block, and one query read a global. In v2 the sim owns its own data and every query takes the world as an argument. The JS lift-out itself is not worth doing. | **redesign** — v2 already plans to replace this comment fence with the Rust simulation core boundary (V2_PHASE0_BRIEF.md:7). The fence is a convention, not dead code, and the v2_note keeps its idea. Its contents are marked row by row (CS1-CS9 inside the fence; the helpers outside it in CS10). |

# Game (`shamble-game.js`)

## GW. Game world, map building and movement physics

Scope: shamble-game.js, several ranges.

| ID | System | What it does | Where | Evidence and numbers | State | v2 note | Mark (recommended) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GW1 | Game manifest and the player's fixed faction and team | The header comment lists what shamble-game.js provides and needs under the classic-script load order. Two constants fix the single local player to the Allies faction and its team id. | shamble-game.js:1-25 (manifest comment), :26 PLAYER_FACTION='Allies', :27 PLAYER_TEAM (TEAMS.find by faction) | none recorded | [C] | The manifest only documents shared globals and load order, so it has nothing to port. PLAYER_FACTION and PLAYER_TEAM build in one local player. In v2 the server assigns every connected player a team and a squad (LOOP_V2 §2: three teams, 12-16 each, squads of 4, bots fill slots). The team id becomes per-connection state, never a global. Many systems read PLAYER_TEAM, such as barracks colours at game:530, so each of those needs a viewer-relative 'my team' on the client. | **redesign** — LOOP_V2 §2 replaces one hard-coded player team with per-player team and squad assignment; the manifest itself is a classic-script artefact. |

In the Rust port these become collider data from the map document and positions in sim state, not mesh transforms. | **rewrite in Rust** — Movement collision is decided by the authoritative sim. |

In v2 the reach follows from the fixed tick, and map validation (the EDITOR_V2 reachability panel, the LOOP_V2 §9 reachability check) must use the integrated value, not the formula. ## GA. Game animation (ANIM v2)

Scope: shamble-game.js:224-573 and the held melee and shield models.

| ID | System | What it does | Where | Evidence and numbers | State | v2 note | Mark (recommended) |

| GA1 | Animation tuning table and critically damped spring | One data object holds every animation knob: gait (duty, pelvis drop, lift, cadence bounds), spring stiffnesses, carry / ADS / sprint hold poses per hold style (rifle, smg, scatter; each gun picks one through its GUN_KIT hold), reload choreography keys, and action poses (flinch, death, draw, bayonet charge, throw, shield, katana). An analytic critically damped spring smooths most blended values and stays stable at any frame time (velocity and throw wind-up use their own exponential smoothing, lines 279 and 370). Module-level scratch vectors, quaternions and arrays are reused every frame, so the layer creates no three.js math objects per frame (no `new` between lines 271 and 444). V8 still allocates boxed floats inside it: AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md CS11 puts the remaining garbage down to V8's inlining budget running out in big hot functions, which leaves float arguments and returns boxed. | shamble-game.js:132-193 const ANIM (hold 153-163, reload 172-177, actions 179-185, aim 187-191); shamble-game.js:197 springStep; scratch pools _aV/_aQ 231 (10 Vector3 + 10 Quaternion), _aE/_aY/_aDn/_aKnee 232, _aPoleR/_aPoleL/_aHold 233, _aRk 237; helpers _aLerp 234, _aWrap 235, _aSmooth 236, _aKeys 238-239, _aNeutral 240 | ANIMATION_PLAN.md §4 documents how holds and reload styles are added as data; no metric of its own recorded | [C] | Keep it as plain data (a JSON or TS const the Anim Lab can export into). springStep is small, allocation-free and frame-rate independent: port it as is. ANIM.aim.carry/ads are dead data (see GA16); only ANIM.aim.adsK is read. Swings move relative to the moving stance spot so a foot never trails. | GA15 | Per-frame animation driver and its input contract | Every frame, the player and every alive bot fill one shared state struct (aim yaw, clock, kick, reload dip, reload progress, throw charge, bayonet lean, grounded, ADS, draw timer) and call updateSoldierAnim. Bots pass no kick, reload, throw, draw or bayonet lean; their ADS comes from a short aim timer (en._aimT). Aim pitch travels outside the struct: pitchWeapon writes the gun's rotation.x (and a bot with no target decays it with rotation.x*=0.9, which scales last frame's posed Euler angle rather than the aim pitch). updateSoldierAnim spots a fresh value by comparing it with the value it stored after last frame's posing, then feeds it into the carry and ADS pitch. The header claims zero per-frame allocation, but the audit measured it as a top allocator (CS11: boxed floats once V8's inlining budget runs out, not object creation): v2 must verify this with a profiler. At 48+ soldiers, plan distance or visibility LOD and update-rate cuts: by arithmetic, 48 soldiers is about 1.2 ms per frame at the runtime figure (~26 µs) and 2.4 ms at the headless figure (51 µs). The 2-bone/ankle solves stay CPU work (main thread, a worker, or a compute pass); GPU skinning would not solve IK, but rigid skinning of the parts would cut the per-part draw calls and Object3D matrix updates that the rig costs today. ## GC. Game combat, teams and vehicles

Scope: shamble-game.js, several ranges.

| ID | System | What it does | Where | Evidence and numbers | State | v2 note | Mark (recommended) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GC1 | Team identity and friendly-fire filter | Every team string goes through one function that maps canonical ids, faction names and the legacy 'friend'/'hostile' to a team id or null; sameTeam compares two of them, and teamFilter turns friendly fire on or off for shot resolution. | shamble-game.js:27 PLAYER_TEAM; :514-517 teamOf; :518 sameTeam; :519 teamFilter; TUNE.ff at shamble-core.js:103 | three-teams-core-steps-1-6.md:87: sameTeam 7.45 ns per call, 0 B, reaches TurboFan. three-teams-core.md: harnesses h6-h13 PASS. STATUS.md:68 (ADR-001 inputs): the server must repeat the team allow-list (teamOf). | [M] | Keep one choke point and exact comparisons only (no 'in' test or object lookup, so prototype keys can never match). Drop the 'friend'/'hostile' aliases once old maps are migrated. The server validates team ids in map data and in client messages. | **rewrite in Rust** — Team filtering decides who a shot or blast can hurt, so the authoritative sim must own it. |
| GC2 | Soldier slot table, team index and roster activation | A fixed table of soldier slots (slot 0 is you, each bot's slot is its SIM id) and a per-team index of HQs, refinery, power, barracks, spawn markers, truck and dock lanes, rebuilt after each map build. activateRoster plays a team when it has an HQ, parks the slots of teams that don't play, assigns AI roles by team slot and colours health bars by their relation to you. | shamble-game.js:714-716 TEAM_INDEX; :717-735 buildTeamIndex; :762-779 activateRoster; :794 SOLDIERS; callers :646 (buildMap), :1839 (resetRound), :3225 (boot) | three-teams-core-steps-1-6.md:31: 12 fixed slots (0 = you, 1-3 allies, 4-7 soviets, 8-11 empire). Review fix: parked slots leave the scene graph (r128 updates the matrices of hidden objects). three-teams-core.md: perfbots at 48 soldiers, combat 0.80-1.00 ms per update step; fairness 2.55 / 2.54 / 2.47 scans per bot per second. | [M] | Keep slot id equal to sim id, a per-team index with no per-frame searches, and a forced re-sync when a slot is parked. LOOP_V2 §2 has three teams with team count and size as data, and VISION_V2 §3 [DECIDED] also keeps two-team modes (2-8 players), with team count and each team's faction as mode data (§3 [DEFAULT], §6 item 1): keep parking the teams a mode does not use (legacy already plays 2 of 3 teams on Cabana), but decide it from the mode definition instead of 'has an HQ' (764-765), and drop the 'else Soviets' fallback. STATUS.md:68: humans take over bot slots. Drop the scene-graph parking and the stylizeOne-on-return step (r128/look workarounds). The health-bar recolouring belongs to the client. | **rewrite in Rust** — The roster and the per-team structure index are core sim state that targeting, damage and objectives read every tick. |
| GC3 | Cursor aim resolution and ADS sight line | Each frame a ray from the camera through the cursor is resolved against the headless SIM up to 400 m, falling back to the ground plane, giving the aim point and whether it rests on an enemy, a structure or a block. While aiming down sights (and not holding the Torque Bow, whose charge arc replaces the line, :1138), a sight line from the animated muzzle is drawn: white over open ground, dark grey on any structure or non-soldier target, red only on an enemy soldier; a yellow line stands from the ground up to the aim height. updateAim also runs the frame's first syncSimWorld (:1125). | shamble-game.js:32 sightLine/heightLine/adsMode; :1109-1111 aim ray state (raycaster, groundPlane, aimPoint/aimGround, aimHoverEnemy/aimPart/aimTarget/aimBlocked; :1110 also declares the shared scratch vectors _v/_p1/_p2, reused outside aiming by throwMolotov :1406, pitchWeapon :1429, enemyFire :1617, spawnDamageNumber :2501 and blastReaches :2543); :1119-1134 updateAim; :1135 _ms/_adir/_aq/_aup scratch; :1136-1150 updateAimVisuals; called at :2707; ADS input at :3284-3285, toggle at :3299; mesh build :3195-3204 (sight line: cylinder with an alpha-fade DataTexture, opaque at the muzzle; height line 0xffd23f) | AUDIT_BUGS_PERF.md:12: updateAim never reaches TurboFan (before CS2; CS2 removed the const-tracking deopts). No dedicated aim measurement recorded. | [M] | Keep aim resolved through the sim's own ray code (crosshair and shot agree) and a red sight line that never shows on an ally. The legacy already rays into the SIM (soldiers, structures, blocks) and into resolveShot's own y=0 ground plane (shamble-core.js:1507); aimGround is used only when nothing is hit within 400 m (1128). So open ground is aimed at height 0, but platform and building tops are aimed at their own height and ramps at the top of their bounding box, above the slope (probe through tools/preview.js eval on the default map, a downward ray through the SIM: it stops at 1.20, 2.50 and 4.00 m on the three platforms, and at 2.50 m on a ramp whose slope there is 1.25 m). Only the throwVelocity users (grenades, Molotovs and the bow: heading to aimGround, 1711) and the Juggernaut cannon (aimGround + 0.6 m, 1304) still aim at the y=0 ground point, so LOOP_V2 §9's 'today the cursor aims at the ground' holds for those only; LOOP_V2 §9 marks aiming at height [OPEN] (ray-cast into the world with gentle aim help), rule pending LOOP_V2 [OPEN]. Legacy aims at a world point, not a direction: each pellet's direction is aimPoint minus playerFireOrigin (1473), and aimPoint is the camera ray's hit in the SIM as the client sees it (1126-1129). So the v2 command should carry the quantised aim point (or the camera ray) with the client's view time; the server rebuilds the direction from its own fire origin and re-resolves the shot against the world rewound to that time. A bare direction would shift the shot by any correction of the fire origin. | **port to TypeScript** — Cursor-to-world mapping and the sight line are client input and feedback; the height-aim rule is pending LOOP_V2 §9 [OPEN]. *Look brief pending.* |

## GP. Game presentation, input and dispatch

Scope: shamble-game.js, several ranges.

| ID | System | What it does | Where | Evidence and numbers | State | v2 note | Mark (recommended) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GP1 | FX pool and transient combat effects | Short-lived world effects: tracers, muzzle flashes, impact sparks by surface, shell casings, explosions, grenade blast (shock rings, dust, shrapnel, scorch, scuffs), the concussion overlay and the camera kick. Every effect except the grenade flash sphere (a new mesh per blast, 1082) is drawn from per-kind pools of meshes that are built once, and one fx[] loop fades them and returns them to the pool. | shamble-game.js:1009-1091 (fx 1010, FXPool 1015-1017, acquireSpark 1019, addTracer 1020, addMuzzle 1021, addImpact 1022, addCasing 1031, addExplosion 1034, concussion 1044, scuffTick 1049, spawnShock 1053, spawnDust 1058, fragBurst 1064, spawnScorch 1075, grenadeBlast 1079); pool defs 3237-3251 (scuff capped at 40, 3249); per-frame tick 2946-2960; FX_SPARK_MAX 24 / FX_FRAG_MAX 64 at 1018 | AUDIT_BUGS_PERF P4: a new Mesh, Geometry and Material per FX event. Fixed by CS6 (AUDIT §7): pooled, scuffs capped at 40 (the peak had been 103), 'visuals A/B-identical; fuzz 0'. TECHNICAL:117: bot-shot FX are skipped beyond TUNE.fx.botCullR 50 m. Code read at 48ffb51 found a live bug at 1050: a `//` comment swallows the rest of the line, so pooled scuffs never get their scale, rotation or position. | [M] | Keep the pool-per-kind design with caps, and keep the fade curves as data (LOOP_V2 §7 feel kit: muzzle flash, tracer and impact per weapon). Avoid three things: the swallowed-comment bug at 1050 (scuffs show at a stale or origin transform, standing upright); the grenade flash, still a new SphereGeometry and Material per blast (1082); and fx.splice per expiry. The camera kick and concussion stay client-only and must never feed aim. The pooling technique carries over; the look is pending. *Look brief pending.* |
| GP2 | Input mapping and listeners | Keyboard and mouse listeners write a keys{} map plus mouse NDC and button flags, and they also run game actions directly at event time, outside update(): R startReload or resetRound (3264), F useAbility (3265), G startCharge (3266) and its keyup releaseCharge, which throws the grenade (3273), E enterJug, exitJug, openShop or tryResupply (3267), 1 and 3 equipPrimary and equipSpecial (3268-3269), the left button's bow charge and the mouseup release that fires the bolt (3284-3285, releaseCharge 1724), and the right button's ADS state (3284-3285). Only movement, sprint, jump, held or queued fire, holding E to channel the tech box (keys['KeyE'] read by updateTechBox at 948, called at 2697) and the cursor aim are sampled inside update(). Hard-coded keys: WASD, Shift, Space, R (reload, or restart the round after game over), F, G, E, 1/2/3, Tab, Esc/P, the backquote key and '['. Middle mouse orbits the camera, the right button aims (hold or toggle). Inputs are released when the tab is hidden, and text selection, drag and the context menu are suppressed. The reticle DOM is placed once per frame, not per mousemove. | shamble-game.js:1094-1101 (keys, mouseNDC, mouseDown, semiQueued, mode, camYaw state, refreshRect); listeners 3252-3294 inside bootGame (keydown 3252, keyup 3273, mousemove 3279, mousedown 3284, mouseup 3285, visibilitychange 3291, wheel 3293); R restart-or-reload branch 3264 (the restart clicks 3305-3306 are GE12's); scroll 3275 refreshRect; audioKick listeners 3358; placeCursor 3045-3052 with its last-position cache _curX/_curY 3044; resize index.html:1263; core makeInputCommand shamble-core.js:1481 (unused by the loop) | gate-check-5 report l.12: smoke test 5a drives real input in headless Chromium (Deploy, then the tutorial, then W must move the player). AUDIT_BUGS_PERF:109 (CS9, dead-code removal) and AUDIT_BUGS_PERF:123 (audit complete) both keep 'makeInputCommand (multiplayer groundwork)' on purpose. A grep finds it called nowhere. No latency or cost numbers recorded. | [C] | Keep three ideas: the cached canvas rect (no layout reads on mousemove), cursor placement once per frame, and the input release on visibilitychange. In v2, input becomes an input command per tick (seq, tick, move, aim and buttons, as in makeInputCommand) sent to the Rust sim, with movement already turned into world axes. The actions legacy runs at event time (reload, ability, grenade throw, bow release, E, weapon switch, ADS) become press and release edges in that command, carried so that a press is neither lost nor doubled when inputs are resent for redundancy. Keep the bindings in one table (today the key codes are raw literals scattered through keydown), so the controls list (GP8) is generated from it. Rebinding is not proposed: no VISION_V2 §6 need asks for it. Digit2 is bound to devCycleGun in the player build (it only acts when DEV.allGuns is set). DEV.allGuns defaults to false (786), so Digit2 and the wheel gun cycle do nothing in a normal player build. keydown carries an editor branch (3255) and wheel carries editor and charlab branches (3293), all guarded by mode; v2 should split them per app. | **port to TypeScript** — Client input mapping. Port the mapping and the hygiene, then emit commands instead of mutating state. |
| GP3 | Player locomotion and sprint stamina | Turns WASD into a move vector relative to the camera yaw, applies sprint (drains stamina; when it runs out, sprint locks until the bar refills to 30 %), ADS, banzai and shield speed multipliers, then smooths the velocity, clamps to the arena and resolves collision. Collision costs are in GW13 (AUDIT P1 resolveObstacles 7.5 % of allocation). | [C] | Keep as data: STAM values, sprintMult per unit, adsMoveMult, shieldMoveMult, accel. The sim should get a world-space move direction (the client applies the camera yaw), because today's axes depend on camYaw (2751). The velocity lerp 1-exp(-accel*dt) assumes a variable dt, so in v2 it runs at the fixed tick. LOOP_V2 has no sprint or stamina rule, so these are legacy starting values. The stamina bar is HUD (GP10). Collision and vertical movement are owned by GW13/GW14. | **rewrite in Rust** — Movement is decided by the authoritative simulation. |
| GP4 | Gameplay camera rig | An isometric follow camera: a screen-space dead zone before the camera pans or looks ahead, smoothed middle-mouse yaw, ADS reaction ('pitch' flattens the view toward the crosshair, 'pan' is a Foxhole-style distance pan that keeps the player on screen), FOV easing toward T.fov/T.fovADS with a per-shot FOV kick (fovKick, set at 1481 and 1804), random positional shake, and a spring-damped concussion kick (impulses at 1089 and 1545, reset at 1849). camRecoil does not move the camera: the firing sites add to it (1410, 1481, 1804), 2838 decays it, and only updateReticle reads it (2514, it widens the reticle gap). STATUS risks: 11 wall-clock reads (performance.now / Date.now) make same-seed matches differ (an ADR-001 input). tools/sim.js calls update(1/60) directly (TECHNICAL:230). | [C] | v2 splits the fixed-tick Rust sim (Worker or server) from a client render loop that interpolates. EDITOR_V2 leaves the Character Lab and Anim Lab out of scope ('stay as they are', EDITOR_V2:4), so where the charlab branch goes is not decided there. resetRound mixes sim reset and HUD writes, which v2 should separate (server resets state, the client re-renders). | **redesign** — The v2 architecture (a Rust sim at a fixed tick, run in a Web Worker or on the server, and a TypeScript render loop with interpolation: V2_PHASE0_BRIEF:121-122, R1 and R2) replaces a variable-dt sim inside rAF. |
| GP14 | update(dt): the per-step simulation order | The body of the game step: timers and cooldowns, the economy trickle and objectives, the aim cast, the player's shots and melee, the player's movement, grenades, a forced SIM re-sync, torque-bow bolts, bots in a rotating fair order with sight and route budgets, building sink, Juggernauts, then the FX loop. HUD writes are interleaved throughout. | shamble-game.js:2671-2965 update (NAV.budget=2 at 2671, _frameId 2672, timers 2675-2690, economy 2692-2698, regen 2700, aim 2706-2707, firing 2722-2731, movement 2748-2780, syncSimWorld(true) 2862, bolts 2863-2885, AI budget and fair order 2889-2893, bots 2893-2941, buildings 2944, Juggernauts and FX 2947-2960) | TECHNICAL §4a (l.94-104): 'The real update(dt) order', marked [verified: shamble-game.js:2671-2947], 'not yet phased (a phased update() is an ADR-001 input)'. AUDIT_BUGS_PERF §1: update() 0.49 ms/frame CPU; P2/CS11: update() stays in V8's baseline tier (~0.5 ms/frame even unoptimized). three-teams-core report l.77: applyDamage runs addTech's HUD writes (ADR-001 debt). | [C] | Make the order explicit phases of the Rust tick, and change the legacy order only on purpose, because the order decides outcomes. The legacy order is: timers, cooldowns, reload and respawn (2675-2690); the economy and objectives (2692-2698); regen (2700); the player's aim (2707), shots (2722-2731) and melee impact (2738-2743), all on positions from before this step's movement; the player's movement (2748-2780); the piloted Juggernaut's drive and cannon fire (tickPilot, 2781); grenades (2856-2859); a forced re-sync (2862) that exists only because the scene is the truth and goes away in v2 (GW16); bolts (2863-2885); bots in fair order, each one steering, then firing, then colliding (2893-2941); building sink (2944); Juggernauts (2947); FX (2948-2960). Keep the fair rotating bot order and the per-step sight and route budgets (PERF-1 / NETCODE-1 at 2890). Strip out of the tick: the HUD writes, performance.now() (_animClock 2674, tickPilot 2781, among the 11 wall-clock reads in STATUS), and Math.random in AI. The individual mechanics are owned by GC, GN, GE, GA and GW. Found in code: STATUS's 11 wall-clock reads are 10 calls plus one in a comment (2169). Two reach sim outcomes, both through animation: tickPilot's clock (2781) bobs the piloted Juggernaut's cannon muzzles (GC17; tickJuggernauts' clock, 2947, only animates idle mechs that do not fire), and _animClock (2674) drives the soldiers' breathing (363), which rotates the chest that carries the weapon (shamble-core.js:453) and so moves the animated muzzle grenades and Molotovs launch from (GC13). The rest change results through the one shared Math.random, which sound and FX draw from inside the step under conditions the sim must not depend on: fireOnce calls sfxShot (1473) before the pellets' spread draws (1477), and sfxShot fills a noise buffer with floor(sampleRate x 0.07-0.2 s; the AMK returns early to sfxAMK) Math.random values only when audio is unlocked and not muted (2227-2231; sfxAMK its own, 2286-2290), so the spread depends on mute and on the device's sample rate; sfxImpact is throttled by the wall clock (2253); sfxEnemyShot (2255-2256) and the FX (1029-1088) are culled by camera distance (GN8); camera shake draws inside update() (2846). A legacy-parity harness must seed Math.random and stub audio and FX, as docs/reports/2026-10-16-three-teams-core.md:39 did; v2 gives the sim its own seeded stream and the client separate cosmetic streams. Order found in code: the player always acts first. Its shots, melee, movement and bolts resolve before any bot, so a bot the player kills this step cannot fire back (applyDamage sets it dead at once, 1520, and it takes the dead branch, 2937), and bots aim at the player's position after its move (re-sync, 2862). With many humans under server authority, the tick needs one defined order for every soldier's command, or all shots of a tick resolved against the same pre-tick state, so that a trade never depends on slot order. | **rewrite in Rust** — This is the authoritative step: its order decides shots, movement and damage. |
| GP15 | bootGame boot sequence | The single entry point both pages call. It builds the world (border 3056, tree and prop kit builders 3059-3188, map choice and load 3189, player weapon props and ADS sight lines 3192-3204, the 12-slot roster 3208-3218, harvesters, tech boxes, silos and objectives 3225-3235, then rules 3236), defines the FX pools, wires every input, menu, career and dev listener, sets the initial HUD, runs stylizeScene once, applies the environment, and warms the nav grid. Despite the manifest ('SIDE-EFFECTS ON LOAD: none', shamble-game.js:24), several top-level statements still build objects, touch the scene and write the DOM at script load, outside bootGame; each belongs to its own system's row, this row records only the boot-order fact. | shamble-game.js:3055-3372 bootGame (applyRules 3236, FXPool defs 3237-3251, listeners 3252-3316, ProgStore load 3317, dev panel 3320-3355, feel systems 3357-3359, initial HUD 3362, stylizeScene 3363, applyEnv 3365, navBuild 3368 plus the warm-up navPath calls 3369-3371 (GP16's)); called at index.html:1259 and editor.html:1261; load-time statements outside bootGame: 66 (comic texture pre-warm, CL12/GW3), 69 (GridHelper added to scene, GW5), 611 (enemySpawnLayer, GW10), 683 (player built and added to scene, CC6; the CS5 TDZ source), 701-702 and 715 (teamEco, TEAM_INDEX fill), 973 (SILO pool), 1099 (reload-ring style, GP10), 1702-1705 (grenade-arc meshes, GC13), 1861-1863 (rank label and pip DOM, GE17), 3379 (let warm-up block, GP16); manifest claim at 24 | TECHNICAL:30 and the game file manifest (shamble-game.js:16-24: 'consolidates all game-boot side-effects; both pages call it'). AUDIT_BUGS_PERF:88-91: during CS5 (the Juggernaut livery change-set), the boot test caught a temporal-dead-zone error: the player is built while the script loads (683) and PLAYER_FACTION was declared later. It now sits at shamble-game.js:26 (START_HERE:133). bot-poplar-wedge report l.15: three warm-up navPath calls at the end of bootGame. | [C] | This exists because classic scripts share globals and need one place for side effects. v2 module initialisation replaces it. Keep the ordering lessons: build the world before stylizeScene, which runs once, while runtime builders call stylizeOne; define pools before first use; no work at module load that depends on later globals (the CS5 TDZ). The manifest's 'SIDE-EFFECTS ON LOAD: none' (24) is false: see the load-time list in where. | **drop** — A classic-script boot shim. v2 modules and an explicit init replace it. |

The code comment at 3370 says 'up to 54 per match', which does not match the report. | [M] | Drop both. They exist only because of classic-script top-level lets and a hot JS pathfinder; v2 runs nav in Rust and has no shared globals. The block is generated by AST and must be rebuilt whenever a top-level let is added. ## TL. Tools (the verification toolkit)

Scope: `tools/`, `.claude/hooks/syntax-gate.mjs`.

| ID | System | What it does | Where | Evidence and numbers | State | v2 note | Mark (recommended) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TL1 | Gate driver (base ref, result, exit codes) | Runs every gate check on the working tree against a base git ref and prints PASS, FAIL or SKIP per check, or JSON. The default base is the newer merge-base of HEAD with main or origin/main. Exit code 2 means a usage or setup error (missing npm dependencies, an unknown base ref, or a 5a smoke without Playwright or Chromium when nothing else failed), so the code was not judged. | tools/gate.mjs:15-100 (usage, argument parsing, resolveBase at :71, baseFile/headFile, OLD_PREFIX :35), :186-223 (smokeCheck, ORDER, finish with exit 0/1/2 at :213, add, smokeOnly path :220), :372-375 (noSmoke path and the final smoke run) | docs/reports/2026-10-14-gate-check-5.md:8 (22 of 22 mutation proofs fail exactly the expected checks), :42 (a stale local main makes the gate use origin/main's base 7c80e56 and print a note), :43 (no Chromium leads to a 5a FAIL with exit 2, and --no-smoke passes), :44-45 (CI: gate job 10 and 17 s; locally about 17 s full and about 2.5 s with --no-smoke); STATUS.md:125 (full gate PASS in 26 s on main 8675367, all 8 checks); STATUS.md:102 at f2ccc13 (GitHub protects nothing on main; PROTECT-MAIN open), stale since 2026-10-17: branch protection requires the gate and smoke checks, as PR #25 records (STATUS.md:102 at 4c24d07) | [M] | Keep: an explicit base ref (newer of local and remote main), exit 2 for a setup problem and never a silent skip, --json output, --allow-drop tied to the approved plan, and proving every gate change with mutations. The README's rule is 'never edit the gate tools to make a check pass'. Keep: the gate is a real guard because branch protection requires it (since 2026-10-17; STATUS.md:102 at 4c24d07). V2_PHASE0_BRIEF R5 (:125) plans a 'gate v2 that replaces today's gate', built on cargo, tsc, Vitest and Playwright. | **redesign** — R5 plans gate v2 to replace this driver. The base-ref, exit-code and mutation-proof rules carry over; the checks it runs do not. |
| TL2 | Checks 1 and 2: syntax and load-order concatenation | Runs node --check on each engine file and each inline page script (check 1). It then joins each page's classic scripts in their <script> order and checks the joined file (check 2). Errors are mapped back to the source file and line. | tools/gate.mjs:104-127 nodeCheck, :131-140 pageScripts, :144-157 classicParts (also refuses a src outside the repo), :225-235 check 1, :237-247 check 2 | docs/reports/2026-10-13-phase-0.md:26 (a syntax error fails check 1 at the right line); gate-check-5.md:27 (all 8 checks PASS on 684a022); STATUS.md:125 | [M] | These checks exist only because the game is classic scripts sharing one global scope (a hard constraint). In v2, cargo build and tsc and Vite module bundling catch both problems. Learn: map every error back to its file and line. | **drop** — A workaround for classic scripts sharing globals; the v2 compilers and bundler do this job. |
| TL3 | Check 3: top-level symbol conservation (battery.js) | Lists every top-level declaration per file with acorn and compares base against head. A symbol dropped without --allow-drop fails, and so does a duplicate across files or within one file. The output lists added, dropped and moved symbols. battery.js also has a command line that prints this as JSON (the official battery). | tools/battery.js:70-104 analyze/topDecls/patternNames (symbols, intraDupes), :208-214 exports and CLI; tools/gate.mjs:249-275 | phase-0.md:25-27 (deleted refreshRect leads to a FAIL [3] DROPPED; wrType copied into core leads to a FAIL [3] cross-file duplicate; --allow-drop refreshRect passes; a pure move passes and is listed as moved), :28 (the battery.js refactor gives byte-identical JSON, sha256 20d1ff0c...); STATUS.md:125 (core 134 / game 691 / editor 349 symbols, measured 2026-10-14 on main 8675367; on today's engine files, unchanged since 48ffb51, node tools/battery.js . gives core 138 / game 714 / editor 353); repo-root.md:16 (+0 / -0) | [M] | It guards the shared-global surface. A dropped global breaks another file at run time without any error before that. With ES modules and Rust crates, the compilers catch missing exports. Learn: an explicit 'what this change-set removes on purpose' list is a good review habit. If v2 wants API-surface tracking at the Rust and TypeScript boundary, that is a new tool, not this one. | **drop** — It exists only because of classic-script globals; module imports and exports make a missing symbol a compile error. |
| TL4 | Check 4: player build never reaches editor-only code | Finds every reference in the player build (core, game, index.html inline script and DOM handlers) to a symbol that only shamble-editor.js declares. It classifies each one: guarded (behind a mode==='editor' or 'charlab' test, an &&, a typeof guard or a switch on mode), dead (unreachable from load-time code), or FLAGGED (would throw in index.html). Uses an AST on purpose (the placeCursor incident). | tools/battery.js:106-205 (editorOnly, localsOf/shadowed, isModeTest/isTypeofGuard/guardOf, reachability over declByName, refs tally); tools/gate.mjs:276-283 | STATUS.md:125-126 (44 editor references, all guarded, 0 flagged, measured 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-14; on today's engine files, unchanged since 48ffb51, node tools/battery.js . gives 51 editor references, 51 guarded, 0 flagged); phase-0.md:25-26 (bootEditor() put in bootGame leads to FAIL [4] FLAGGED); phase-0.md:39-40 (the verifier found two holes, a trailing <script> and single-quoted handlers, fixed in 607b04b) | [M] | In v2 the editor is its own module or entry point. EDITOR_V2 §5 (:51-52): 'The player build (index.html) must never load editor code'; the editor is 'a natural first module'. A bundler import graph or lint rule then enforces this. Learn the rule, not the mode-guard heuristics. | **redesign** — EDITOR_V2 §5 (:51-52) keeps the rule (the player build never loads editor code) and plans the editor as its own module; R5's gate v2 (V2_PHASE0_BRIEF:125) enforces it as an entry-point / import-graph check. The mode-guard, typeof and reachability heuristics are legacy-only and do not carry over. |
| TL5 | Shared HTML scanner (script tags, attributes, inline handlers) | Splits a page the way a browser does: comments and script or style bodies are not markup. It returns each <script> with its attributes and body offset, reads attributes in any quoting, classifies script type (classic, module or data) and lists inline on...= handlers with their lines. Gate checks 1, 2, 5b and 5d use it, and so does battery.js. | tools/battery.js:18-19 unentity, :23-38 scanHtml, :40-41 attrsOf, :43-44 JS_MIME/scriptKind, :46-47 inlineOf, :49-63 handlersOf | gate-check-5.md:18 (one HTML scanner shared with the gate), :38-41 (unquoted onclick and unquoted <script src> FAIL as they should; a commented-out script tag, an on... variable and '</script >' PASS as controls) | [M] | Needed only because the pages hold inline scripts and inline DOM handlers. Avoid both in v2 (they also block a strict content security policy: STATUS:99 defers SRI/CSP to hosting). Known gap: the syntax hook (TL10) does not use this scanner. | **drop** — A legacy-only workaround for inline scripts and on...= handlers, which the Vite TypeScript client will not have. |
| TL6 | Check 5b: every bare name resolves (plus the gate-globals.json allowlist) | Runs eslint-scope over each page's scripts and inline handlers. Every free name must resolve to a top-level declaration of that page's build, an ECMAScript built-in, or a name listed in gate-globals.json (14 browser globals plus THREE). It also fails on an assignment to a const and on any THREE.<member> that three r128 lacks (for example CapsuleGeometry). | tools/gate.mjs:159-184 scopeOf/scoped, :285-336 check 5b (BUILTINS from a pristine vm context :287, R128 member list :291); tools/gate-globals.json:1-22 | gate-check-5.md:33-38 (mutations: a bare localStorage and status, THREE.CapsuleGeometry, a const assignment, an implicit global and a typo in code the smoke never reaches all FAIL 5b), :53-54 and README:38-40 (strict by design: Annex B block functions and direct eval FAIL); STATUS.md:104 (gate.mjs:333 and README:34 call gate-globals.json 'reviewed data', which contradicts kit-v2-1 category 3; wording, deferred) | [M] | TypeScript strict mode, the DOM lib setting and eslint no-undef or no-restricted-globals replace it. Keep the lesson: an explicit, reviewed allowlist of browser globals, so a typo that happens to be a window property (name, status, event) fails. The r128 member check goes with r128. Built-ins depend on the Node version that runs the check, so pin the version (CI pins 22). | **drop** — Needed only because classic scripts resolve names at run time; the v2 type checker does this at compile time. |
| TL7 | Check 5c: core layering | Fails if shamble-core.js references any name declared in game, editor or an inline page script, even behind typeof. This keeps the engine layer standalone. | tools/gate.mjs:338-347 | gate-check-5.md:38 (mutation 'core calls game' fails 5c); STATUS.md:125 (5c PASS on main) | [M] | The rule carries over and gets bigger: the Rust sim core must not depend on the client, the TypeScript client must reach the sim only through the Wasm boundary, and the editor must not leak into the player build. The engine-architect owns these dependency rules. In v2 they are enforced by crate and package boundaries plus an import-graph lint, not by scope analysis over globals. | **redesign** — The layering rule stays in v2, but R5's gate v2 enforces it through crate and module boundaries. |
| TL8 | Check 5d: exact script order | Fails unless each page loads exactly three r128 from cdnjs, then core, then game (then editor, on editor.html), then one inline script, with no async, defer, nomodule or type=module. | tools/gate.mjs:349-370 (THREE_URL, WANT) | gate-check-5.md:34 (core and game tags swapped, and defer on the game script, both fail 5a and 5d), :38-39 (an unquoted src and a text/ecmascript editor tag fail 5d) | [M] | In v2, modules and the bundler set load order. Lesson for the gate: the TDZ-at-load and wrong-order bugs it caught also need the smoke test (TL9) to show up. | **drop** — It exists only because of classic scripts in a fixed load order and the three r128 CDN tag. |
| TL9 | Check 5a: smoke test of the real pages (Playwright) | Opens index.html and editor.html in headless Chromium (SwiftShader), serves three r128 locally and drives them with real clicks and keys. index.html: menu, Deploy, close the tutorial, hold W (the player must move at least 0.1 m), fire two shots (both clicks must reach the canvas), about 5 s of play, Esc to pause, back to the menu. editor.html: the prefab list and map list must fill. It fails on any page error, console error, network request, WebSocket or file read outside the repo. | tools/smoke.mjs:30-39 until/press, :40-114 SESSIONS, :118-193 runSmoke (network and WebSocket routing :147-157, step runner :159-165, renderer string :170-173), :196-208 CLI | gate-check-5.md:27-29 (5/5 clean runs at 16.7-19.3 s, the player moved 0.9-1.6 m), :31-32 (TDZ at load, a throw in deploy(), dead movement, a WebSocket and an outside file read all fail 5a only), :68 (flakiness: one wait took 9.4 s under load, so the limit is now 30 s), :44-45 (CI smoke job 57-59 s, about 20 s of it apt); STATUS.md:125 (8/8 and 5/5 steps) | [M] | Keep the shape: wait on game state with generous timeouts rather than on timing, check that each step did its job, treat console errors as failures, report the renderer string, and use a fresh storage context for each page. Change: the probes read legacy globals (mode, started, player, tutorialOpen), so v2 needs a small test-hook API instead. The network rule flips: allow the local test server's WebTransport and WebSocket, block everything else. Errors more than about 1 s after the last step are not seen (README). R5 already lists Playwright. | **redesign** — R5 (V2_PHASE0_BRIEF:125) plans a gate v2, with Playwright among its tools, that replaces today's gate, which runs this as check 5a. The harness shape carries over (state waits, per-step checks, console errors and blocked network as failures, renderer string, fresh storage per page). The probes move to a v2 test-hook API, and the network rule flips to allow the local game server's WebTransport and WebSocket. |
| TL10 | Per-edit syntax hook | A Claude Code PostToolUse hook on Edit and Write. ## Not covered and not verified

- **core** (138 top-level declarations): 138 are named in some row's Where; none is left uncovered.
- **game** (714 top-level declarations): 625 are named in some row's Where; the other 89 sit inside a line range a row cites (mostly scratch vectors, DOM handles and small helpers of that row's system). A range hit is mechanical: it shows a row covers the lines, not that the row describes each name; none is left uncovered.
- **editor** (353 top-level declarations): 293 are named in some row's Where; the other 60 sit inside a line range a row cites (mostly scratch vectors, DOM handles and small helpers of that row's system). A range hit is mechanical: it shows a row covers the lines, not that the row describes each name; none is left uncovered.
- **Merged duplicates** (2): CC7: duplicate of CL5 (the same applyVillain, core:268-277); its lesson, its evidence and its 'drop' view were folded into CL5 on 2026-10-27; CM14: duplicate of CC12 (the same MELEE_IDLE, makeSword, makeAxe and makeShield, core:1434-1472); its extra call sites (game:1658, :2743) and its cosmetic-only note were folded into CC12 on 2026-10-27.
- **Measurements** ([M] rows) were read at their source, not re-run, except where a row says so (for example the
  headless evals in CC4 and CC12). Some `docs/ANIMATION_PLAN.md` §6 metrics come from headless snippets that are not
  checked into `tools/`, so they cannot be re-run as they are.
- **The map editor** has summary rows here (ED); its features are listed row by row in `docs/EDITOR_V1_INVENTORY.md`.
- **Routines** (KT rows) are as the routines API showed them; settings that only the claude.ai web app shows were not
  read.
- **Marks** are recommendations; none is a decision until Mustafa confirms it.
- **The look**: no row decides it; the look pipeline row says "redesign (look brief pending)" and nothing more.
